{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_012RTeCPaQMBfDwT4TmPbUke","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→# VersaSuite — Product Research\n     2→\n     3→Researched: 2026-02-15\n     4→Developer website: https://www.versasuite.com\n     5→\n     6→## Overview\n     7→\n     8→VersaSuite is a comprehensive healthcare information technology platform developed by Universal Software Solutions Inc. (USSI), a privately held company founded in 1994 and headquartered in Austin, Texas. The company has been doing business as \"VersaSuite\" since the early 2000s. The product has been designed and programmed by a single core development team in Austin since June 1997. VersaSuite positions itself as an all-in-one HIS/EHR/EPM/ERP solution — a combined Hospital Information System, Electronic Health Record, Enterprise Practice Management, and Enterprise Resource Planning platform — running on a single database and single framework. They claim 36 integrated modules.\n     9→\n    10→VersaSuite primarily targets small to mid-size hospitals, including critical access hospitals, specialty hospitals (surgical, behavioral health, LTAC, rehabilitation, hospice), acute care facilities, and ambulatory/outpatient clinics. Their ONC certification SED user description explicitly says \"Small hospitals.\" The company appears to be a small, niche vendor — no customer counts are disclosed publicly, and reviews across platforms (Capterra, G2, SoftwareFinder) are extremely sparse (1-3 reviews total). No KLAS data was found. The product is available as both a desktop client-server application (VersaSuite) and a web-based platform (VersaWeb), and supports English and Spanish interfaces. The company reports serving clinics, surgical centers, and hospitals globally, though specifics are not disclosed.\n    11→\n    12→Notable integration partners mentioned on third-party listings include AxiaMed (payment processing), Microsoft, TruCode (coding), Acuant (identity verification), CVS Caremark (pharmacy), and Eyemaginations (patient education).\n    13→\n    14→## Product: VersaSuite\n    15→\n    16→CHPL ID: 10299 (VersaSuite 9.0, certified 2020-02-10)\n    17→\n    18→### What It Is\n    19→\n    20→VersaSuite is a monolithic, all-in-one healthcare IT platform covering clinical, administrative, and financial functions for both inpatient and outpatient settings. The certified module appears to be the entire product — the company repeatedly emphasizes that it is \"ONE product, built on ONE Framework, running on ONE Database.\" There are not separate, independently deployable products; rather, it is a single platform with modules that can be configured for different facility types and specialties.\n    21→\n    22→The product is available on two delivery platforms: a Windows desktop client-server application and VersaWeb, a modern web-based interface. Both share the same database and core functionality.\n    23→\n    24→### Users & Market\n    25→\n    26→**Target facilities:**\n    27→- Critical access hospitals (a primary marketing focus)\n    28→- Specialty hospitals (surgical, behavioral health, LTAC, rehabilitation, hospice)\n    29→- Acute care hospitals\n    30→- Ambulatory/outpatient clinics and practices\n    31→- Urgent care facilities\n    32→- Ambulatory surgical centers\n    33→- FQHCs and RHCs (mentioned on feature pages)\n    34→- Home health\n    35→- Dental practices (mentioned as a supported specialty)\n    36→\n    37→**End users:** Physicians, nurses, clinical staff, pharmacists, lab technicians, radiology technicians, billing staff, HIM professionals, administrative/financial staff, HR personnel, and patients (via patient portal).\n    38→\n    39→**Market position:** VersaSuite is a small, niche vendor. No customer counts, revenue figures, or installation base numbers are publicly disclosed. Review data is minimal — Capterra shows a small number of reviews (approximately 3), with positive feedback emphasizing the product's flexibility, comprehensive feature set, and strong support. One reviewer (a Director of Informatics at a critical access hospital) praised the training and cost-effectiveness. Another noted a steep learning curve and occasional software glitches. No KLAS reviews or significant analyst coverage was found. The vendor does not appear to have significant market share compared to larger competitors.\n    40→\n    41→### Modules & Functionality\n    42→\n    43→VersaSuite claims 36 modules across four integrated systems. Based on vendor website content and third-party listings, these include:\n    44→\n    45→**Clinical / EHR:**\n    46→- **Electronic Health Records (EHR)** — Clinical documentation with specialty-specific templates. Supports ambulatory, ED, and inpatient environments with the same interface. Features advanced drawing components, keyboard-free data entry, voice dictation integration (Dragon Medical, M*Modal), auto-generated exam summaries, graphical data visualization, and concurrent multi-patient record access.\n    47→- **Computerized Physician Order Entry (CPOE)** — Order management for medications, lab, and diagnostic imaging.\n    48→- **Electronic Medication Administration Record (EMAR)** — Medication administration tracking.\n    49→- **Electronic Treatment Administration Record (ETAR)** — Treatment authorization and administration.\n    50→- **Clinical Decision Support** — Implied by (a)(2) certification for clinical decision support interventions.\n    51→- **Emergency Department Information System (EDIS)** — Emergency department workflows (mentioned on specialty hospitals page).\n    52→\n    53→**Laboratory & Imaging:**\n    54→- **Laboratory Information Management System (LIMS)** — Lab test ordering, processing, and results management.\n    55→- **Radiology Information System / PACS (RIS/PACS)** — Radiology workflow management and picture archiving/communication. One-click access from within the EHR.\n    56→\n    57→**Pharmacy:**\n    58→- **Pharmacy Information Management System** — Medication management, formulary management. Electronic prescribing (certified for (b)(3) electronic prescribing). Integration with CVS Caremark noted.\n    59→\n    60→**Practice Management & Billing:**\n    61→- **Enterprise Practice Management (EPM)** — Scheduling, registration, check-in, billing, accounts receivable, collections. The outpatient page describes automating \"every aspect of any ambulatory clinic from scheduling and check-in through accounts receivable and collections.\"\n    62→- **Point of Sale (POS)** — Payment/transaction processing at point of service.\n    63→- **Automatic billing code transmission** — Billing codes pass automatically from clinical to financial systems.\n    64→\n    65→**Health Information Management:**\n    66→- **Health Information Management (HIM)** — Medical records management, coding. Integration with TruCode for coding noted.\n    67→- **Document Management System (DMS)** — Records and file storage/retrieval.\n    68→\n    69→**Patient & Provider Portals:**\n    70→- **Patient Portal** — Patient access to health information, including FHIR API access (certified for (e)(1) view/download/transmit and (g)(10) FHIR API). Pricing noted as $150/provider/year outpatient, $50/bed/year inpatient.\n    71→- **Provider Portal** — Clinician workflow tools.\n    72→\n    73→**Nutrition:**\n    74→- **Clinical Nutrition & Food** — Dietary/nutritional services management.\n    75→\n    76→**Administrative / ERP:**\n    77→- **Accounting / General Ledger** — Financial management, accounts receivable/payable reconciliation.\n    78→- **Payroll** — Employee compensation processing.\n    79→- **Human Resources (HR)** — Employee management, onboarding, evaluations.\n    80→- **Employee Time & Attendance** — Time tracking, overtime, tardy tracking.\n    81→- **Employee Scheduling** — Workforce scheduling.\n    82→- **Employee Portal** — Self-service for employees to access personnel folders, PTO, direct deposit, communicate with management.\n    83→- **Budgeting** — Financial planning.\n    84→- **Facility Management** — Operations management.\n    85→- **Procurement** — Purchasing functions.\n    86→- **Inventory & Materials Management** — Supply chain operations.\n    87→- **Risk and Compliance Management** — Regulatory oversight.\n    88→\n    89→**Reporting & Analytics:**\n    90→- **Reports Module** — Custom reporting, data analytics, quality reporting. Access to \"discrete and sensitive variables.\"\n    91→- **Reports/Letters** — Clinical and administrative documentation generation.\n    92→\n    93→**Public Health Reporting:**\n    94→- Certified for (f)(1) through (f)(7) — immunization registries, electronic case reporting, public health syndromic surveillance, cancer case reporting, and other public health reporting.\n    95→\n    96→**Interoperability:**\n    97→- Certified for (b)(1) transitions of care, (b)(2) clinical information reconciliation, and (b)(3) electronic prescribing.\n    98→- FHIR API support via (g)(7), (g)(9), and (g)(10) certification.\n    99→- Direct messaging for transitions of care implied by (h)(1) certification.\n   100→\n   101→### Data & Content\n   102→\n   103→Based on the modules, features, certifications, and vendor descriptions, VersaSuite manages an extremely broad range of data types across a single integrated database:\n   104→\n   105→**Clinical data (explicitly described (immunizations, syndromic surveillance, cancer cases, etc.)\n   147→\n   148→**Key architectural note:** The vendor's core marketing message is that all of this data resides in a single database. This is significant for EHI export assessment — if the product truly uses one database, then a comprehensive export mechanism should be able to access all clinical, administrative, and financial data without crossing system boundaries.\n   149→\n   150→**Gaps / Uncertainty:**\n   151→- No specific mention of patient messaging or secure messaging beyond the employee portal communication feature and patient portal. Access to \"discrete and sensitive variables.\"\n- **Reports/Letters** — Clinical and administrative documentation generation.\n\n**Public Health Reporting:**\n- Certified for (f)(1) through (f)(7) — immunization registries, electronic case reporting, public health syndromic surveillance, cancer case reporting, and other public health reporting.\n\n**Interoperability:**\n- Certified for (b)(1) These files primarily consist of data stored in CSV (Comma-Separated Values) format. Additionally, any information stored in other formats, such as images and documents, will also be included as saved files. The exported files are organized and saved to patient-specific folders within VersaSuite's default export folder.</p>\n    69→\n    70→    <h4>Key Points to Note:</h4>\n    71→\n    72→    <ol>\n    73→        <li><strong>CSV Data:</strong> The primary data exported by the ehi export tool is provided in CSV files. These files contain structured data in a tabular format, making it easy to manipulate and analyze.</li>\n    74→        <li><strong>Images and Documents:</strong> Any information, such as images and documents, associated with patient records will also be exported. These files are included in their original format.</li>\n    75→        <li><strong>Patient-Specific Folders:</strong> Exported data is organized into patient-specific folders. Each patient's data is stored separately within these folders for easy access and management.</li>\n    76→        <li><strong>Data Dictionary:</strong> The documentation accompanying the export tool provides a data dictionary for the tables that may be exported. This dictionary serves as a reference guide, describing the structure and meaning of the exported data fields.</li>\n    77→        <li><strong>Selective Export:</strong> If a particular table does not contain any patient data, it will not be exported. This ensures that only relevant and meaningful information is included in the export.</li>\n    78→    </ol>\n    79→\n    80→    <p>The ehi export tool in VersaSuite simplifies the process of exporting electronic health information, allowing you to access patient data in a structured and organized manner. Our focus here aligns with Regulation §170.315 (b)(10), addressing both single patient and patient population EHI export functionalities within our health IT solutions.</p>\n        \n        <h3>VersaSuite's Commitment</h3>\n        <p>At VersaSuite, we are committed to advancing healthcare technology while adhering to the latest standards in interoperability, information blocking prevention, and certification requirements set forth by the Office of the National Coordinator for Health Information Technology (ONC). Our documentation offers a detailed and user-friendly guide to understanding and utilizing our EHI export features, ensuring compliance and efficiency in healthcare data management.</p>\n        \n        <h4>Key Aspects of Regulation §170.315 (b)(10):</h4>\n        <ol>\n            <li><strong>Single Patient EHI Export:</strong>\n                <ul>\n                    <li>Enables a streamlined export of a single patient's comprehensive EHI.</li>\n                    <li>Designed for ease of use without the need for developer assistance.</li>\n                    <li>Access control is implemented, limited to specific users or system administrators.</li>\n                    <li>Exports are provided in an electronic, computable format with a publicly accessible hyperlink detailing the export's format.</li>\n                </ul>\n            </li>\n            <li><strong>Patient Population EHI Export:</strong>\n                <ul>\n                    <li>Facilitates the export of EHI for an entire patient population.</li>\n                    <li>Ensures that exports are in a computable, electronic format.</li>\n                    <li>Each export file includes a publicly accessible hyperlink explaining the export format.</li>\n                </ul>\n            </li>\n        </ol>\n\n        <h4>Documentation Overview:</h4>\n        <p>VersaSuite's documentation portal is structured to provide a clear, comprehensive view of each data element within our EHR systems. These files primarily consist of data stored in CSV (Comma-Separated Values) format. Additionally, any information stored in other formats, such as images and documents, will also be included as saved files. The exported files are organized and saved to patient-specific folders within VersaSuite's default export folder.</p>\n\n    <h4>Key Points to Note:</h4>\n\n    <ol>\n        <li><strong>CSV Data:</strong> The primary data exported by the ehi export tool is provided in CSV files. These files contain structured data in a tabular format, making it easy to manipulate and analyze.</li>\n        <li><strong>Images and Documents:</strong> Any information, such as images and documents, associated with patient records will also be exported. These files are included in their original format.</li>\n        <li><strong>Patient-Specific Folders:</strong> Exported data is organized into patient-specific folders. Each patient's data is stored separately within these folders for easy access and management.</li>\n        <li><strong>Data Dictionary:</strong> The documentation accompanying the export tool provides a data dictionary for the tables that may be exported. This dictionary serves as a reference guide, describing the structure and meaning of the exported data fields.</li>\n        <li><strong>Selective Export:</strong> If a particular table does not contain any patient data, it will not be exported. This ensures that only relevant and meaningful information is included in the export.</li>\n    </ol>\n\n    <p>The ehi export tool in VersaSuite simplifies the process of exporting electronic health information, allowing you to access patient data in a structured and organized manner. fileName.replace(/\\.html$/, \"\");\n  // Try to extract a table-like name (AP_xxx, HC_xxx, etc.)\n  const match = name.match(/\\b(AP_\\w+|AX_\\w+|HC_\\w+|EM_\\w+|FQ_\\w+|VE_\\w+)\\b/);\n  if (match) return match[1];\n  // Otherwise use cleaned file name\n  return name;\n}\n\nfunction inferCsvName(html: string, fileName: string): string | null {\n  // Look for \"Documentation for X.csv\" pattern\n html.match(/(?:AP|AX|HC|EM|FQ|VE)_\\w+\\.csv/);\n  if (tableMatch) return tableMatch[0];\n  return null;\n}\n\nfunction extractTitle(html: string): string | null {\n  // Get first h1 or h2\n  const h1Match = html.match(/<h1[^>]*>(.*?)<\\/h1>/is);\n  const h2Match = html.match(/<h2[^>]*>(.*?)<\\/h2>/is);\n  const title = h1Match?.[1] || h2Match?.[1] || null;\n  if (title) return !== \"Original Column Name\") {\n      columns.push({\n        original_name: original,\n        expanded_name: expanded,\n        description: desc,\n      });\n    }\n  }\n  return columns;\n}\n\nfunction isPlaceholderDescription(desc: string): boolean {\n  const lower = desc.toLowerCase();\n  return (\n    lower.startsWith(\"provides information related to\") ||\n    lower.startsWith(\"contains detailed information or identifiers for the corresponding\") ||\n    lower.startsWith(\"specifies the date associated with the\") ||\n    lower.startsWith(\"records the historical data or changes related to the\") ||\n    lower === \"\"\n  );\n}\n\nfunction assessDescriptionQuality(columns: Column[]): \"good\" | \"mixed\" | \"poor\" {\n  if (columns.length === 0) return \"poor\";\n  const placeholderCount = columns.filter((c) =>\n    isPlaceholderDescription(c.description)\n  ).length;\n  const ratio = placeholderCount / columns.length;\n  if (ratio < 0.2) return \"good\";\n  if (ratio < 0.6) return \"mixed\";\n  return \"poor\";\n}\n\nfunction inferPrefix(tableName: string | null): string | null {\n  if (!tableName) return null;\n  const match = tableName.match(/^(AP_|AX_|HC_|EM_|FQ_|VE_)/);\n  return match ? match[1] : null;\n}\n\nfunction inferDomain(prefix: string | null, title: string | null): string | null {\n  if (prefix && PREFIX_DOMAINS[prefix]) return PREFIX_DOMAINS[prefix];\n  if (title) {\n    const lower = title.toLowerCase();\n    if (lower.includes(\"billing\") || lower.includes(\"statement\") || lower.includes(\"charge\"))\n      return \"Billing/Financial\";\n    if (lower.includes(\"clinical\") || lower.includes(\"patient\") || lower.includes(\"encounter\"))\n      return \"Healthcare/Clinical\";\n    if (lower.includes(\"address\") || lower.includes(\"contact\") || lower.includes(\"phone\"))\n      return \"Administrative/Patient\";\n    if (lower.includes(\"lab\")) inferTableName(file);\n    const title = extractTitle(html);\n    const csvName = inferCsvName(html, file);\n    const prefix = inferPrefix(tableName);\n    const domain = inferDomain(prefix, title);\n    const quality = assessDescriptionQuality(columns);\n\n    const placeholderCount = columns.filter((c) =>\n      isPlaceholderDescription(c.description)\n    ).length;\n    totalPlaceholderDescs += placeholderCount;\n\n    tables.push({\n      file_name: file,\n      table_name: tableName,\n      page_title: title,\n      csv_file_name: csvName,\n      prefix,\n      domain_hint: domain,\n      columns,\n      column_count: columns.length,\n      has_patient_id: columns.some(\n        (c) => c.original_name === \"PtID\" || c.original_name === \"PatientID\"\n      ),\n      has_encounter_id: columns.some(\n        (c) => c.original_name === \"PtEncID\" || c.original_name === \"EncounterID\"\n      ),\n      has_audit_fields: columns.some((c) => c.original_name.startsWith(\"ZU\")),\n      description_quality: quality,\n    });\n  } catch (err: any) {\n    failures.push({ file, error: err.message });\n  }\n}\n\n// Build summary\nconst prefixDist: Record<string, number> = {};\nconst domainDist: Record<string, number> = {};\nconst qualityDist: Record<string, number> = { good: 0, mixed: 0, poor: 0 };\n\nfor (const t of tables) {\n qualityDist[t.description_quality]++;\n}\n\nconst totalColumns = tables.reduce((s, t) => s + t.column_count, 0);\n\nconst summary: Summary = {\n  total_files_discovered: allFiles.length,\n  total_files_parsed: tables.length,\n  parse_failures: failures,\n  index_file_skipped: allFiles.includes(\"index.html\"),\n  tables_extracted: tables.length,\n  total_columns: totalColumns,\n  columns_with_placeholder_descriptions: totalPlaceholderDescs,\n  description_quality_distribution: qualityDist,\n  prefix_distribution: prefixDist,\n  domain_distribution: domainDist,\n  tables_with_patient_id: tables.filter((t) => t.has_patient_id).length,\n  tables_with_encounter_id: tables.filter((t) => t.has_encounter_id).length,\n};\n\n// Write outputs\nwriteFileSync(\n  join(OUT_DIR, \"data-dictionary.json\"),\n  JSON.stringify(tables, null, 2)\n);\nwriteFileSync(\n  join(OUT_DIR, \"data-dictionary-summary.json\"),\n  JSON.stringify(summary, null, Served by Microsoft-IIS/10.0 with ASP.NET. Last modified 2025-10-10. No redirects.\n\n**Step 2: Fetch and examine the page**\n\n```bash\ncurl -sL \"https://docs.versasuite.com\" -H 'User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0' -o page.html\n```\n\nThe page is a single static HTML document titled \"VersaSuite - Electronic Health Information Export Documentation.\" It contains:\n1. An introductory section explaining the EHI export in the context of §170.315(b)(10)\n2. A description of the export tool and its CSV-based output format\n3. A data dictionary index listing 96 individual HTML pages, each documenting one exported CSV table\n\n**Step 3: Download all linked data dictionary pages**\n\nEach link is a relative URL to an HTML file on the same domain (e.g., `HC_Pt.html`, `AP_ContactEntry.html`). Downloaded all 96 linked pages:\n\n```bash\n# For each file linked from the index:\ncurl -sL \"https://docs.versasuite.com/{filename}\" -H 'User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0' -o \"{filename}\"\n```\n\nAll 96 pages downloaded successfully (97 total including index). No anti-bot measures, no JavaScript required, no authentication. Total download size: ~660 KB.\n\n**Step 4: No additional documentation found**\n\nThe site is purely the index page with data dictionary links. There are no:\n- Downloadable PDFs, ZIPs, or other file formats\n- Subpages or navigation beyond the data dictionary\n- API documentation endpoints\n- User guides or instructional materials beyond the brief overview on the index page\n- Schema files (XSD, JSON Schema, DDL)\n- Sample export files\n\n## What Was Found\n\n### Export Mechanism\n\nVersaSuite's EHI export tool produces a **CSV-based export** organized into patient-specific folders within a default export directory. The export includes:\n\n1. **CSV files** — one per database table, containing structured tabular data\n2. **Images and documents** — associated patient files exported in their original format (images, scanned documents, etc.)\n3. **Selective export** — tables without patient data for a given patient are omitted\n\nThe export supports both single-patient and patient-population modes, as described on the main page.\n\n### Data Dictionary\n\nThe documentation consists of 96 HTML pages, each documenting one CSV table that may appear in an export. Each page provides a three-column table:\n\n| Column | Content |\n|--------|---------|\n| Original Column Name | The CSV column header (database field name) |\n| Expanded Column Name | A human-readable expansion of the abbreviation |\n| Description | A text description of the field's purpose |\n\n**No data types are documented** — there is no indication of whether fields are integers, strings, dates, booleans, or coded values. **No value sets are documented** — for fields that reference coded values (e.g., `BillingTypeID`, `MaritalStatus`, `MedStatusID`), the valid values are not listed. **No foreign key relationships are formally documented** — while many tables share `PtID` and `PtEncID` columns, the cross-table relationships are not explicitly stated.\n\n### Table Prefixes and Domains\n\nThe 96 tables span six naming prefixes that map to functional domains:\n\n| Prefix | Count | Domain |\n|--------|-------|--------|\n| HC_ | 25 | Healthcare/Clinical — patient records, billing, labs, medications, problems, appointments |\n| AP_ | 24 | Administrative/Patient — demographics, contacts, addresses, alerts, images, tasks |\n| AX_ | 13 | Accounting/Financial — general ledger, journal entries, invoices, purchase orders, inventory |\n| VE_ | 12 | EHR Exam/Template — clinical note entries, template documents, data storage tables |\n| EM_ | 7 | Encounter Management — admissions, treatment administration, medication review |\n| FQ_ | 2 | Financial/Quality — patient earnings, patient financial info |\n| (none) | 13 | Mixed — clinical notes, encounter docs, patient history, billing statements, attachments |\n\n### Key Statistics\n\n- **96 tables** documented across all domains\n- **2,381 total columns** across all tables\n- **42 tables** (44%) contain a `PtID` (Patient ID) field\n- **18 tables** (19%) contain a `PtEncID` (Patient Encounter ID) field\n- **All tables** include ZU audit fields (`ZUChkSum`, `ZUDate`, `ZULogonHisID`)\n\n### Description Quality\n\n- **86 tables** (90%) have \"good\" quality descriptions — meaningful, specific text\n- **10 tables** (10%) have \"poor\" quality descriptions — dominated by placeholder text\n- **458 of 2,381 columns** (19.2%) use placeholder descriptions like \"Provides information related to...\" or \"Contains detailed information or identifiers for the corresponding...\"\n\nThe placeholder descriptions are concentrated in the larger clinical tables (HC_DrugRxHis at 88 columns, HC_LabC at 118 columns, HC_DrugHisAT at 84 columns) — ironically the most data-rich tables have the worst documentation.\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\nVersaSuite is documented as a comprehensive HIS/EHR/EPM/ERP platform with 36 integrated modules running on a single database. Comparing the product research against the 96 exported tables:\n\n**Clearly covered domains:**\n- **Patient demographics** — `HC_Pt` (83 columns) is comprehensive: name, DOB, SSN, sex, marital status, contact info, insurance assignment, consent dates, provider assignments\n- **Patient encounters/admissions** — `EM_AdminHis` (110 columns, the largest table) covers admission/encounter details; `Patient_Encounter_dataset_documentation` (56 columns) provides encounter-level data; `PtTransfer_dataset_documentation` covers transfers\n- **Medications/prescriptions** — `HC_DrugRxHis` (88 columns) covers drug prescription history including NDC codes, dosing, routes, frequencies, e-prescribing status; `EM_TreatAdminHis` (43 columns) covers treatment administration\n- **Laboratory** — `HC_LabC` (118 columns, second largest) covers lab results in detail; `HC_LabSpec` (69 columns) covers lab specimens\n- **Clinical notes** — `Clinical_Notes_dataset_documentation` (20 columns) covers note metadata; `VE_CN` and `VE_CNEntry` cover clinical note content; `VE_TmplDocHis` covers template documents\n- **Problems/diagnoses** — `HC_ProblemHis_documentation` (28 columns) covers problem history\n- **Allergies** — `AP_AllergyHis_Documentation` (23 columns) and `Healthcare Allergy Reconciliation History` (15 columns)\n- **Patient billing/balances** — `HC_PtBal_documentation` (21 columns) with aging balances; 5 `HC_BlngStmt*` tables covering billing statements in detail (charge forms, DOS, summaries, aging balances)\n- **Appointments** — `HC_ApptInfoMain` (69 columns), `HC_Appt_Documentation` (29 columns), `HC_ApptTrail_Documentation` (49 columns)\n- **Contacts/addresses** — `AP_ContactEntry`, `AP_PhoneEntry`, `Address Entry Information`, plus history tables for each\n- **Images/documents** — `AP_Img_expanded` (42 columns) covers image/document metadata; `Attachment Information` (11 columns)\n- **Behavioral health** — `HC_BHCP` (19 columns) covers behavioral health care plans\n- **Blood bank** — `HC_BBDonor` (10 columns), `Healthcare - Blood Test Encounter Status` (7 columns)\n- **Continuity of care** — `HC_ContOfCare` (17 columns)\n- **Accounting/GL** — `AX_GL` (27 columns), `AX_JE` (30 columns), `AX_JEEnt` (31 columns) and related financial tables\n- **Inventory/procurement** — `AX_InvtInvoice`, `AX_InvtInvoiceAct`, `AX_ItemPO`, `AX_ItemPR`\n- **PHI requests** — `HC_PHIReq` (37 columns) covers patient health information disclosure requests\n- **Medication administration** — `EM_MedReviewHis`, `EM_MedSignoffHis`, `HC_DrugHisAT` (84 columns for medication administration tracking)\n- **Payroll/direct deposit** — `Direct Deposit Transactions Data Dictionary` (10 columns)\n- **EHR exam data** — 9 `VE_Data_*` tables storing different data types (dates, integers, text of various lengths, timestamps)\n\n**Notable gaps or unclear coverage:**\n- **Vital signs** — No dedicated vitals table is documented. Vital signs may be stored in the generic `VE_Data_*` tables (which store EHR exam template data by data type), but this is not explicitly documented.\n- **Immunizations** — No immunization-specific table despite the product being certified for (f)(1) immunization registry reporting. Immunization data may be within the medication or encounter tables, but is not separately identified.\n- **Procedures/surgical records** — No dedicated procedures table. The product is used by surgical hospitals and ambulatory surgical centers, yet there is no explicit surgical/procedure documentation.\n- **Radiology/imaging results** — Despite having a RIS/PACS module, there is no radiology-specific table in the data dictionary. Imaging results may be in the generic encounter or template data tables.\n- **Care plans** — Only behavioral health care plans (`HC_BHCP`) are documented. No general care plan table exists.\n- **Referrals** — No referral-specific table, though `HC_Pt.NoReferal` tracks referral counts.\n- **Insurance/enrollment** — While billing balances and claim-related fields exist in `HC_Pt`, there is no dedicated insurance coverage or enrollment table.\n- **Patient portal data** — `HC_Pt.PortalID` references the portal, but there is no table for portal communications or patient-generated data.\n- **Nutrition/dietary** — Despite a Clinical Nutrition & Food module, no dietary data table appears.\n- **E-prescribing details** — The drug prescription table has eRx flags, but no separate e-prescribing transaction table.\n\n**Important note on the VE_Data_* tables:** The EHR exam data is stored in a generic entity-attribute-value (EAV) pattern across 9 tables differentiated by data type (`VE_Data_DL` for date-long, `VE_Data_DT` for date-time, `VE_Data_I1`/`I2`/`I4` for integers of different sizes, `VE_Data_T030`/`T100`/`T255` for text of different lengths, `VE_Data_TM` for time). These tables each have the same structure: `DataID`, `TmplDocHisID`, `ObjectID`, `Value`, plus audit fields. This EAV structure means that much of the clinical examination data (likely including vitals, review of systems, physical exam findings, assessment/plan) is stored in these generic tables — but without the template definitions that map `ObjectID` to specific clinical concepts, the data is essentially opaque. The template definitions themselves are NOT included in the export documentation.\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\nThe export is a **proprietary CSV format** — not FHIR, not C-CDA, not any recognized healthcare data standard. This is appropriate and actually better-suited for a (b)(10) export than a FHIR-only approach would be, because:\n\n1. CSV can represent any tabular data, not just data that maps to FHIR resources\n2. The export appears to be a near-direct dump of database tables, which preserves the full data model\n3. It includes financial/accounting data (AX_ tables) that would not fit FHIR\n\nHowever, the CSV export has significant limitations for data portability:\n- **No formal schema** — no DDL, XSD, or JSON Schema. The data dictionary HTML pages are the only structural documentation.\n- **No data types** — column types (string, integer, date, boolean, decimal) are not documented\n- **No value sets** — coded values (IDs referencing lookup tables) are unexplained. Many fields end in `ID` (e.g., `DrugRouteID`, `MedStatusID`, `BillingTypeID`) but the valid values and their meanings are not documented.\n- **No relationship documentation** — foreign keys between tables are implicit (shared column names like `PtID`, `PtEncID`) but not formally specified\n- **No lookup/reference tables** — the export includes transactional tables but appears to omit the reference/lookup tables that decode ID values. Without knowing what `DrugRouteID = 3` means, the data is incomplete.\n\nThis is a **critical gap**: the export gives you data with hundreds of coded ID fields, but doesn't export the code tables that give those IDs meaning. A third party receiving this export could see that a patient has `DrugRouteID = 5` but would have no way to know if that means oral, intravenous, subcutaneous, etc.\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\n**Strengths:**\n- The documentation is publicly accessible at a stable URL with no authentication\n- The static HTML format is simple and machine-readable\n- 90% of tables have good-quality descriptions\n- The index page clearly explains the export mechanism (CSV + images/documents, patient-specific folders)\n- Coverage of 96 tables is relatively broad\n\n**Weaknesses:**\n- **No data types documented** — this is a fundamental omission for a data dictionary\n- **No value sets or code tables** — makes the export data incomplete without the source system\n- **No relationship documentation** — table joins must be inferred from column naming conventions\n- **No sample data** — no example export files are provided\n- **No export procedure documentation** — the index page mentions the \"ehi export tool\" but provides no instructions, screenshots, or step-by-step guide for performing an export\n- **Placeholder descriptions** — 19.2% of all columns have unhelpful template descriptions, concentrated in the most complex clinical tables (labs, medications, drug administration)\n- **The largest, most important clinical tables have the worst documentation** — HC_LabC (118 cols), HC_DrugRxHis (88 cols), HC_DrugHisAT (84 cols) all have \"poor\" description quality\n- **No documentation of the EAV template structure** — the VE_Data_* tables store much clinical exam data, but without template definitions, the data is unusable\n- **The HTML pages lack consistent structure** — some have `<html>` wrappers, some are bare fragments, with inconsistent formatting\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\n- **Granularity**: Field-level documentation with column names and descriptions, but missing data types and constraints\n- **Coded fields**: Extensively used (hundreds of `*ID` columns) but value sets are completely undocumented\n- **Relationships**: Implicit through shared column names only; no ER diagram or relationship specification\n- **Versioning**: Last-modified date on the server (2025-10-10) but no version number or change history in the documentation itself\n\n### Overall Assessment\n\nVersaSuite has made a genuine effort at (b)(10) compliance. The export is CSV-based (not just repackaged FHIR), covers clinical, administrative, and financial data domains, and the documentation is publicly accessible. The breadth of 96 tables across 6 functional domains reflects the product's single-database architecture.\n\nHowever, the documentation has significant gaps that would prevent a third party from fully interpreting the exported data:\n\n1. **The absence of lookup/reference tables** means that coded values throughout the export cannot be decoded. This is the single largest gap.\n2. **The missing template definitions** for the EAV-structured exam data (`VE_Data_*` tables) means a substantial portion of clinical data would be opaque.\n3. **No data types, no value sets, no formal schema** makes automated import essentially impossible without significant reverse-engineering.\n4. **Placeholder descriptions in the most important clinical tables** undermine the documentation's utility where it matters most.\n\nThe documentation reads as a compliance artifact that was generated (likely with AI assistance, given the templated description patterns) rather than a carefully maintained data dictionary designed for interoperability. It satisfies the letter of the (b)(10) requirement (publicly accessible format documentation) but falls short of enabling practical data portability.\n\n## Access Summary\n- Final URL (after redirects): https://docs.versasuite.com\n- Status: found\n- Required browser: no\n- Navigation complexity: direct_link (single index page with 96 linked data dictionary pages)\n- Anti-bot issues: none\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\n\nNone. The documentation was straightforward to access and download. No authentication, no JavaScript rendering, no anti-bot measures, no broken links.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":3051,"cache_read_input_tokens":74485,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":3051},"output_tokens":3,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"6650554b-dc6e-48c5-8816-53c1b41b30f3","uuid":"f93cb6cb-9981-404c-84d4-eadba3624e92"}

A data dictionary index listing 96 individual HTML pages, each documenting one exported CSV table\n\n**Step 3: Download all linked data dictionary pages**\n\nEach link is a relative URL to an HTML file on the same domain (e.g., `HC_Pt.html`, `AP_ContactEntry.html`). No anti-bot measures, no JavaScript required, no authentication. Total download size: ~660 KB.\n\n**Step 4: No additional documentation found**\n\nThe site is purely the index page with data dictionary links. There are no:\n- Downloadable PDFs, ZIPs, or other file formats\n- Subpages or navigation beyond the data dictionary\n- API documentation endpoints\n- User guides or instructional materials beyond the brief overview on the index page\n- Schema files (XSD, JSON Schema, DDL)\n- Sample export files\n\n## What Was Found\n\n### Export Mechanism\n\nVersaSuite's EHI export tool produces a **CSV-based export** organized into patient-specific folders within a default export directory. The export includes:\n\n1. **CSV files** — one per database table, containing structured tabular data\n2. **Images and documents** — associated patient files exported in their original format (images, scanned documents, etc.)\n3. **Selective export** — tables without patient data for a given patient are omitted\n\nThe export supports both single-patient and patient-population modes, as described on the main page.\n\n### Data Dictionary\n\nThe documentation consists of 96 HTML pages, each documenting one CSV table that may appear in an export. Each page provides a three-column table:\n\n| Column | Content |\n|--------|---------|\n| Original Column Name | The CSV column header (database field name) |\n| Expanded Column Name | A human-readable expansion of the abbreviation |\n| Description | A text description of the field's purpose |\n\n**No data types are documented** — there is no indication of whether fields are integers, strings, dates, booleans, or coded values. **No value sets are documented** — for fields that reference coded values (e.g., `BillingTypeID`, `MaritalStatus`, `MedStatusID`), the valid values are not listed. **No foreign key relationships are formally documented** — while many tables share `PtID` and `PtEncID` columns, the cross-table relationships are not explicitly stated.\n\n### Table Prefixes and Domains\n\nThe 96 tables span six naming prefixes that map to functional domains:\n\n| Prefix | Count | Domain |\n|--------|-------|--------|\n| HC_ | 25 | Healthcare/Clinical — patient records, billing, labs, medications, problems, appointments |\n| AP_ | 24 | Administrative/Patient — demographics, contacts, addresses, alerts, images, tasks |\n| AX_ | 13 | Accounting/Financial — general ledger, journal entries, invoices, purchase orders, inventory |\n| VE_ | 12 | EHR Exam/Template — clinical note entries, template documents, data storage tables |\n| EM_ | 7 | Encounter Management — admissions, treatment administration, medication review |\n| FQ_ | 2 | Financial/Quality — patient earnings, patient financial info |\n| (none) | 13 | Mixed — clinical notes, encounter docs, patient history, billing statements, attachments |\n\n### Key Statistics\n\n- **96 tables** documented across all domains\n- **2,381 total columns** across all tables\n- **42 tables** (44%) contain a `PtID` (Patient ID) field\n- **18 tables** (19%) contain a `PtEncID` (Patient Encounter ID) field\n- **All tables** include ZU audit fields (`ZUChkSum`, `ZUDate`, `ZULogonHisID`)\n\n### Description Quality\n\n- **86 tables** (90%) have \"good\" quality descriptions — meaningful, specific text\n- **10 tables** (10%) have \"poor\" quality descriptions — dominated by placeholder text\n- **458 of 2,381 columns** (19.2%) use placeholder descriptions like \"Provides information related to...\" or \"Contains detailed information or identifiers for the corresponding...\"\n\nThe placeholder descriptions are concentrated in the larger clinical tables (HC_DrugRxHis at 88 columns, HC_LabC at 118 columns, HC_DrugHisAT at 84 columns) — ironically the most data-rich tables have the worst documentation.\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\nVersaSuite is documented as a comprehensive HIS/EHR/EPM/ERP platform with 36 integrated modules running on a single database. Comparing the product research against the 96 exported tables:\n\n**Clearly covered domains:**\n- **Patient demographics** — `HC_Pt` (83 columns) is comprehensive: name, DOB, SSN, sex, marital status, contact info, insurance assignment, consent dates, provider assignments\n- **Patient encounters/admissions** — `EM_AdminHis` (110 columns, the largest table) covers admission/encounter details; `Patient_Encounter_dataset_documentation` (56 columns) provides encounter-level data; `PtTransfer_dataset_documentation` covers transfers\n- **Medications/prescriptions** — `HC_DrugRxHis` (88 columns) covers drug prescription history including NDC codes, dosing, routes, frequencies, e-prescribing status; `EM_TreatAdminHis` (43 columns) covers treatment administration\n- **Laboratory** — `HC_LabC` (118 columns, second largest) covers lab results in detail; `HC_LabSpec` (69 columns) covers lab specimens\n- **Clinical notes** — `Clinical_Notes_dataset_documentation` (20 columns) covers note metadata; `VE_CN` and `VE_CNEntry` cover clinical note content; `VE_TmplDocHis` covers template documents\n- **Problems/diagnoses** — `HC_ProblemHis_documentation` (28 columns) covers problem history\n- **Allergies** — `AP_AllergyHis_Documentation` (23 columns) and `Healthcare Allergy Reconciliation History` (15 columns)\n- **Patient billing/balances** — `HC_PtBal_documentation` (21 columns) with aging balances; 5 `HC_BlngStmt*` tables covering billing statements in detail (charge forms, DOS, summaries, aging balances)\n- **Appointments** — `HC_ApptInfoMain` (69 columns), `HC_Appt_Documentation` (29 columns), `HC_ApptTrail_Documentation` (49 columns)\n- **Contacts/addresses** — `AP_ContactEntry`, `AP_PhoneEntry`, `Address Entry Information`, plus history tables for each\n- **Images/documents** — `AP_Img_expanded` (42 columns) covers image/document metadata; `Attachment Information` (11 columns)\n- **Behavioral health** — `HC_BHCP` (19 columns) covers behavioral health care plans\n- **Blood bank** — `HC_BBDonor` (10 columns), `Healthcare - Blood Test Encounter Status` (7 columns)\n- **Continuity of care** — `HC_ContOfCare` (17 columns)\n- **Accounting/GL** — `AX_GL` (27 columns), `AX_JE` (30 columns), `AX_JEEnt` (31 columns) and related financial tables\n- **Inventory/procurement** — `AX_InvtInvoice`, `AX_InvtInvoiceAct`, `AX_ItemPO`, `AX_ItemPR`\n- **PHI requests** — `HC_PHIReq` (37 columns) covers patient health information disclosure requests\n- **Medication administration** — `EM_MedReviewHis`, `EM_MedSignoffHis`, `HC_DrugHisAT` (84 columns for medication administration tracking)\n- **Payroll/direct deposit** — `Direct Deposit Transactions Data Dictionary` (10 columns)\n- **EHR exam data** — 9 `VE_Data_*` tables storing different data types (dates, integers, text of various lengths, timestamps)\n\n**Notable gaps or unclear coverage:**\n- **Vital signs** — No dedicated vitals table is documented. Vital signs may be stored in the generic `VE_Data_*` tables (which store EHR exam template data by data type), but this is not explicitly documented.\n- **Immunizations** — No immunization-specific table despite the product being certified for (f)(1) immunization registry reporting. Immunization data may be within the medication or encounter tables, but is not separately identified.\n- **Procedures/surgical records** — No dedicated procedures table. The product is used by surgical hospitals and ambulatory surgical centers, yet there is no explicit surgical/procedure documentation.\n- **Radiology/imaging results** — Despite having a RIS/PACS module, there is no radiology-specific table in the data dictionary. Imaging results may be in the generic encounter or template data tables.\n- **Care plans** — Only behavioral health care plans (`HC_BHCP`) are documented. No general care plan table exists.\n- **Referrals** — No referral-specific table, though `HC_Pt.NoReferal` tracks referral counts.\n- **Insurance/enrollment** — While billing balances and claim-related fields exist in `HC_Pt`, there is no dedicated insurance coverage or enrollment table.\n- **Patient portal data** — `HC_Pt.PortalID` references the portal, but there is no table for portal communications or patient-generated data.\n- **Nutrition/dietary** — Despite a Clinical Nutrition & Food module, no dietary data table appears.\n- **E-prescribing details** — The drug prescription table has eRx flags, but no separate e-prescribing transaction table.\n\n**Important note on the VE_Data_* tables:** The EHR exam data is stored in a generic entity-attribute-value (EAV) pattern across 9 tables differentiated by data type (`VE_Data_DL` for date-long, `VE_Data_DT` for date-time, `VE_Data_I1`/`I2`/`I4` for integers of different sizes, `VE_Data_T030`/`T100`/`T255` for text of different lengths, `VE_Data_TM` for time). These tables each have the same structure: `DataID`, `TmplDocHisID`, `ObjectID`, `Value`, plus audit fields. This EAV structure means that much of the clinical examination data (likely including vitals, review of systems, physical exam findings, assessment/plan) is stored in these generic tables — but without the template definitions that map `ObjectID` to specific clinical concepts, the data is essentially opaque. The template definitions themselves are NOT included in the export documentation.\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\nThe export is a **proprietary CSV format** — not FHIR, not C-CDA, not any recognized healthcare data standard. This is appropriate and actually better-suited for a (b)(10) export than a FHIR-only approach would be, because:\n\n1. CSV can represent any tabular data, not just data that maps to FHIR resources\n2. The export appears to be a near-direct dump of database tables, which preserves the full data model\n3. It includes financial/accounting data (AX_ tables) that would not fit FHIR\n\nHowever, the CSV export has significant limitations for data portability:\n- **No formal schema** — no DDL, XSD, or JSON Schema. The data dictionary HTML pages are the only structural documentation.\n- **No data types** — column types (string, integer, date, boolean, decimal) are not documented\n- **No value sets** — coded values (IDs referencing lookup tables) are unexplained. Many fields end in `ID` (e.g., `DrugRouteID`, `MedStatusID`, `BillingTypeID`) but the valid values and their meanings are not documented.\n- **No relationship documentation** — foreign keys between tables are implicit (shared column names like `PtID`, `PtEncID`) but not formally specified\n- **No lookup/reference tables** — the export includes transactional tables but appears to omit the reference/lookup tables that decode ID values. Without knowing what `DrugRouteID = 3` means, the data is incomplete.\n\nThis is a **critical gap**: the export gives you data with hundreds of coded ID fields, but doesn't export the code tables that give those IDs meaning. A third party receiving this export could see that a patient has `DrugRouteID = 5` but would have no way to know if that means oral, intravenous, subcutaneous, etc.\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\n**Strengths:**\n- The documentation is publicly accessible at a stable URL with no authentication\n- The static HTML format is simple and machine-readable\n- 90% of tables have good-quality descriptions\n- The index page clearly explains the export mechanism (CSV + images/documents, patient-specific folders)\n- Coverage of 96 tables is relatively broad\n\n**Weaknesses:**\n- **No data types documented** — this is a fundamental omission for a data dictionary\n- **No value sets or code tables** — makes the export data incomplete without the source system\n- **No relationship documentation** — table joins must be inferred from column naming conventions\n- **No sample data** — no example export files are provided\n- **No export procedure documentation** — the index page mentions the \"ehi export tool\" but provides no instructions, screenshots, or step-by-step guide for performing an export\n- **Placeholder descriptions** — 19.2% of all columns have unhelpful template descriptions, concentrated in the most complex clinical tables (labs, medications, drug administration)\n- **The largest, most important clinical tables have the worst documentation** — HC_LabC (118 cols), HC_DrugRxHis (88 cols), HC_DrugHisAT (84 cols) all have \"poor\" description quality\n- **No documentation of the EAV template structure** — the VE_Data_* tables store much clinical exam data, but without template definitions, the data is unusable\n- **The HTML pages lack consistent structure** — some have `<html>` wrappers, some are bare fragments, with inconsistent formatting\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\n- **Granularity**: Field-level documentation with column names and descriptions, but missing data types and constraints\n- **Coded fields**: Extensively used (hundreds of `*ID` columns) but value sets are completely undocumented\n- **Relationships**: Implicit through shared column names only; no ER diagram or relationship specification\n- **Versioning**: Last-modified date on the server (2025-10-10) but no version number or change history in the documentation itself\n\n### Overall Assessment\n\nVersaSuite has made a genuine effort at (b)(10) compliance. The export is CSV-based (not just repackaged FHIR), covers clinical, administrative, and financial data domains, and the documentation is publicly accessible. The breadth of 96 tables across 6 functional domains reflects the product's single-database architecture.\n\nHowever, the documentation has significant gaps that would prevent a third party from fully interpreting the exported data:\n\n1. **The absence of lookup/reference tables** means that coded values throughout the export cannot be decoded. This is the single largest gap.\n2. **The missing template definitions** for the EAV-structured exam data (`VE_Data_*` tables) means a substantial portion of clinical data would be opaque.\n3. **No data types, no value sets, no formal schema** makes automated import essentially impossible without significant reverse-engineering.\n4. **Placeholder descriptions in the most important clinical tables** undermine the documentation's utility where it matters most.\n\nThe documentation reads as a compliance artifact that was generated (likely with AI assistance, given the templated description patterns) rather than a carefully maintained data dictionary designed for interoperability. It satisfies the letter of the (b)(10) requirement (publicly accessible format documentation) but falls short of enabling practical data portability.\n\n## Access Summary\n- Final URL (after redirects): https://docs.versasuite.com\n- Status: found\n- Required browser: no\n- Navigation complexity: direct_link (single index page with 96 linked data dictionary pages)\n- Anti-bot issues: none\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\n\nNone. The documentation was straightforward to access and download. No authentication, no JavaScript rendering, no anti-bot measures, no broken links.\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
